  * The Rocket Balloon has rockets attached to it, hence the name, that give it an increased speed for the first four seconds after deployment. This speed boost is equivalent to that of a level 5 Haste Spell . Aside from that, Rocket Balloons have slightly higher damage and are slightly faster than regular Balloons once the speed boost wears off, but have the same hitpoints as Balloons of the same level.
  * Alternatively, you can use Rocket Balloons to snipe defenses that might ruin your attack, or to trigger traps and reveal Hidden Tesla locations.
  * Rocket Balloons have considerably higher damage upon death than regular Balloons. In most cases, you will usually need less Rocket Balloons than normal Balloons to take out a defense completely. For example, against a relative-level Hidden Tesla , the Hidden Tesla is able to survive one attack of a normal Balloon plus its death damage, but not one attack of a Rocket Balloon and its death damage.
  * Rocket Balloons may be more effective than regular Balloons as a defensive Clan Castle troop. Their significantly increased movement speed drastically reduces the attacker's reaction time when dealing with them, which can lead to the Rocket Balloons quickly dealing massive damage to large groups of troops, and force an early activation of the Archer Queen's or Grand Warden's respective abilities.

## Super Dragon  (rev 613580)
- info: Preferred Target: Any; Attack Type: Area Splash; Housing Space: 40; Movement Speed: 14; Time Between Shots: 0.192s; Time Between Bursts: 1.8s; Shots per Burst: 10; Range: 3 tiles; Special Ability: Roast
- info: Boost Cost: 25,000; Boost Duration: 3d; Dragon Level Required: 7
- info: Preferred Target: None; Attack Type: Area Splash (Ground & Air); Housing Space: 40; Movement Speed: 10; Time Between Shots: 0.192s; Time Between Bursts: 1.8s; Range: 3 tiles; Special Ability: Roast
- stats: Level | Damage per Second | Damage per Attack | Hitpoints | Town Hall Level Required
    10 | 476 | 170.95 | 7,200 | 15
    11 | 496 | 178.12 | 7,600 | 16
    12 | 516 | 185.29 | 8,000 | 17
    13 | 536 | 192.46 | 8,400 | 18
- stats: Level | Damage per Second | Damage per Hit | Hitpoints
    2 | 438 | 165.6 | 6,300
    3 | 476 | 180 | 6,800
    4 | 514 | 194.4 | 7,200
    5 | 552 | 208.8 | 7,600
  * The Super Dragon is a stronger version of the Dragon that shoots bursts of flames, similar to the Roaster in the Builder Base. His flame bursts hit 10 times, each doing splash damage over a sizable area of effect (about 1-tile radius), with a 5x5 area with 21 tiles - an exact square with 4 corners removed.
  * The Super Dragon appears similarly to his regular counterpart, but is larger and his entire body is made out of molten lava, which includes his wings, tail and head. His wing flaps are made out of fire. Instead of breathing out fire once every 1.25s, he attacks with a burst of flames once every 0.192s, with a delay time between bursts of 1.8 seconds.
  * Super Dragons have no preferred target when attacking; they will simply attack the closest building . However, if they become aware of enemy Clan Castle troops , Heroes , or Skeleton Trap skeletons (either by being attacked themselves or by being near another friendly troop under attack), and can fight back against them, they will leave their previously targeted building and instead engage the enemy troops. Once all of the nearby enemy troops are defeated, the Super Dragons will proceed to attack the nearest structure.
  * Just as with Dragons and Electro Dragons, one should consider supporting Super Dragons with funneling, either by using Heroes or replacing a Super Dragon or two with funneling troops. Having Balloons alongside them to trigger traps or to focus on defenses may also prove to be worth it.
  * In terms of spell support, Super Dragons not only appreciate Rage Spells (see above), but also Haste Spells (to compensate their slow speed) and Freeze Spells to freeze or the Lightning Spell to stun threatening defenses such as Air Defenses and Single-Target Inferno Towers . Bat Spells can also help by distracting both these defenses, whereas the Skeleton Spell will only distract the latter defense.
  * The Super Dragon's splash damage only affects multiple structures if they are directly adjacent to each other (no tiles in between them). As such, it will be wise not to have your base buildings touching one another in order to minimize the destructive power of the Super Dragon.
  * The Super Dragon functions mostly the same as his ordinary counterpart, the Dragon - but with more hitpoints and higher damage, in exchange of taking up more housing space. Therefore, the usual counters against aerial units, if placed strategically, continue to provide adequate defense against him, such as Air Defenses , Seeking Air Mines , and Air Sweepers .
  * Super Dragons can prove to be threatening Clan Castle troops, even if only one can be fit inside. Their flame bursts have a sizable area of effect, they are tanky to take out, they are capable of quickly defeating smaller units, and they can deal significant damage to higher-health units like other Dragons, Lava Hounds , or the Archer Queen .
  * Never put Air Defenses too close to each other, otherwise the attacker will get a lot of value from destroying them by using ZapQuake or the Super Dragon's splash burst damage!

## Inferno Dragon  (rev 594174)

    11 | 88 | 178 | 1,780 | 2,500

  * The Inferno Dragon is a super version of the Baby Dragon and has an identical appearance to him, in which he has a metallic helmet with sharp jaw at the bottom connected to a wooden barrel with iron rings and an iron base at the end by a hose. He attacks with an inferno beam from his mouth that builds up damage over time, much like the single-target Inferno Tower .
  * Inferno Dragons have no preferred target when attacking; they will simply attack the closest building . However, if they become aware of enemy Clan Castle troops , Heroes , or Skeleton Trap skeletons (either by being attacked themselves or by being near another friendly troop under attack), and can fight back against them, they will leave their previously targeted building and instead engage the enemy troops. Once all of the nearby enemy troops are defeated, the Inferno Dragons will proceed to attack the nearest structure.
  * Inferno Dragons can serve very well as part of air attacks, as they have the capability of taking down high health buildings quickly. They also have no loss of DPS when next to other troops as well. This is especially good for LavaLoon attacks, where you can send them with the main army and let them take down the defending Archer Queen and Royal Champion , allowing other troops to be used elsewhere (E.G. allowing a Queen Charge to target something else like Town Hall .)
  * This is especially good for LavaLoon attacks, where you can send them with the main army and let them take down the defending Archer Queen and Royal Champion , allowing other troops to be used elsewhere (E.G. allowing a Queen Charge to target something else like Town Hall .)
  * The Air Sweeper should be a defense to watch out for when using Inferno Dragons. The Air Sweeper's pushback will send the Inferno Dragon out of range of his target, resetting his damage build-up.
  * Inferno Dragons can be quite poor at taking out moving targets, particularly a defending Barbarian King , as they will often need to retarget whenever these targets move, resetting the Inferno Dragon's damage build-up. However, against defending Archer Queens and Royal Champions, multiple Inferno Dragons can easily defeat them because the defending Heroes will usually stay in place.
  * Like their non-super counterparts, they can be used to create funnels as well, but this is only better than a Baby Dragon when there are a number of high-hitpoint buildings, due to his less space-efficient health and lower low-level DPS.
  * Against a defending Inferno Dragon, bring a Lightning Spell . It resets the charge and deals a good amount of damage, along with one-shotting other commonly used Clan Castle troops.
  * Inferno Dragons are superb to use as a defensive Clan Castle troop. Their ramping damage acts as movable Inferno Towers on defense. A Freeze Spell or Poison Spell is almost always required to save the tanks and Heroes from this troop.
  * Inferno Dragons are slowed down by low HP buildings. Putting them around your Heroes can buy time before they are targeted by the Inferno Dragon. Also put them around high damage buildings like Air Defense , so they can take the Inferno Dragon out or do major damage to him before they get destroyed.
  * Also put them around high damage buildings like Air Defense , so they can take the Inferno Dragon out or do major damage to him before they get destroyed.
  * An Air Sweeper is detrimental to the Inferno Dragons. Protect them well and make them cover the whole base to destroy Inferno Dragons.
  * Inferno Dragons are far less health efficient than their counterpart. Focus on making high damage areas to waste their space taken.

## Super Yeti  (rev 623186)
- info: Preferred Target: Any; Attack Type: Melee (Ground Only); Housing Space: 35; Movement Speed: 12; Attack Speed: 1s; Barracks Level Required: 14; Range: 0.8 tiles; Special Ability: Shock and Awe
- info: Boost Cost: 25,000; Boost Duration: 3d; Yeti Level Required: 3
- stats: Level | Damage per Second | Damage per Attack | Hitpoints | Electromites spawned
    5 | 465 | 465 | 6,200 | 11
    6 | 495 | 495 | 6,600 | 12
    7 | 525 | 525 | 7,000 | 13
    8 | 550 | 550 | 7,400 | 14
  * The Super Yeti is similar in appearance to the regular Yeti, except he is hairier, has a beard and glowing eyes, and his horns are much larger. He has darker hair, which are jagged his head; his beard is held together by a golden band. He also wears copper gloves with a Super Troop symbol on each one, a yellow pointed mask, and his basket is now colored brown and holds Electromites. He attacks by swiping one of his hands or clobbers the target with both.
  * The Super Yeti is similar to the Yeti, except with higher hitpoints and damage, and spawns one Electromite after taking 400 damage and subsequently every 800 damage thereafter. Electromites act similarly to the Yetimite , but do chain lightning damage and prioritize Buildings in general instead of Defenses .
  * Super Yetis have no preferred target when attacking; they will simply attack the closest building . However, if they become aware of enemy Clan Castle troops , Heroes , or Skeleton Trap skeletons (either by being attacked themselves or by being near another friendly troop under attack), and can fight back against them, they will leave their previously targeted building and instead engage the enemy troops. Once all of the nearby enemy troops are defeated, the Super Yetis will proceed to attack the nearest structure.
  * Super Yetis' above-average health and relatively high attack can help the funnel of the main army, especially when the Electromites that spawn have a deceptively large amount of health to distract other defenses, they can jump over Walls and do a large amount of damage to defenses, making the funnel even smoother.
  * Ensuring that a Super Yeti gets hit enough to activate Electromites is essential to increase the damage output of Super Yeti, since Yeti does not deal a lot of damage by himself, but if Super Yetis get destroyed, then Electromites can be destroyed easily.
  * Due to the high damage output of Super Yetis, a common and viable strategy is to fill the Battle Blimp with Clan Castle Super Yeti, then send in the Blimp to drop the Yetis on key defenses such as Inferno Towers , Eagle Artillery , or the Town Hall itself, paired with a Rage Spell and Clone Spell . The resulting Electromites will help destroy any Buildings where it drops as the lightning chains through the core of the base.
  * To prevent his Electromites from getting chain value, spread your buildings two tiles away from each other. This principle applies to major defenses such as Scattershots , merged defenses, and the Eagle Artillery (or Inferno Artillery if at Town Hall 17).

## Super Minion  (rev 620782)
- info: Preferred Target: None; Attack Type: Single Target; Housing Space: 12; Movement Speed: 16; Attack Speed: 1s; Range: 4 tiles (Normal) 10.25 tiles (Long Shot); Special Ability: Long Shot
- info: Boost Cost: 25,000; Boost Duration: 3d; Minion Level Required: 8
- stats: Level | Damage per Second | Damage per Shot | Hitpoints | Town Hall Level Required
    11 | 360 | 360 | 1,800 | 15
    12 | 370 | 370 | 1,900 | 16
    13 | 385 | 385 | 2,100 | 17
    14 | 400 | 400 | 2,300 | 18
  * The Super Minion is a much larger Minion who has a ring at the middle of his left horn, wears fingerless gloves and a belt going up to its right, and has hands no longer covered in Dark Elixir. It wields a rocket launcher, on which it uses to fire rockets from a long distance to attack. Its Long Shot ability enables it to fire its first 8 shots (first 3 when on defense) at a very long range. Unlike the regular Minion, the Super Minion can be detected and attacked by Seeking Air Mines .
  * Super Minions have no preferred target when attacking; they will simply attack the closest building . However, if they become aware of enemy Clan Castle troops , Heroes , or Skeleton Trap skeletons (either by being attacked themselves or by being near another friendly troop under attack), and can fight back against them, they will leave their previously targeted building and instead engage the enemy troops. Once all of the nearby enemy troops are defeated, the Super Minions will proceed to attack the nearest structure.
  * Consider using a Rage Spell to maximize the amount of damage your Super Minions do with their long-distance attacks. With some precision, you can place a Rage Spell so that the Super Minions will be in its area of effect from a distance and up close, getting the most value out of it.
  * However, they can be targeted by Inferno Artillery , in addition to dealing splash damage and damage over time.
  * These long-range attacks also allow them to effectively attack defending the Archer Queen , Grand Warden , and Royal Champion without them fighting back. Two Super Minions are generally enough to defeat any of the above heroes if defenses, other buildings or troops do not get in the way.
  * Once the Long Shots run out, the Super Minion will also need some time to move to the shorter-range position from which it attacks thereafter, during which it is vulnerable to the mercy of defenses. Because of its slow movement speed, if you are not already using a Rage Spell as above to buff its speed, you can also use a Haste Spell to get it to its short-range position quickly.
  * For example, combining Super Minions with the Headhunters in defense will be able to effectively shut down unprepared Queen Walks or Charges. Such a Queen Walk/Charge will need at least two spells to deal with both groups (e.g. Poison to slow down the Super Minions, and a Freeze Spell to stop the Headhunters from doing massive damage to the Archer Queen), and due to her low health recovery, the Super Minions can easily pierce through the her ability and stop a successful attack.
  * It can be handy to distract the Super Minion with unimportant buildings such as Barracks , Army Camps and possibly resource collectors and storages depending on the situation) so that their Long Shot abilities are wasted on them. Defend these buildings to prevent attackers from utilizing other troops like Archers to clear these buildings. Distracting Skeleton Trap skeletons are also useful in this regard, especially if the attacker triggers it by another air troop in a way that the Super Minion uses a Long Shot from far enough to attack the Skeletons.
  * An Air Sweeper can prove useful against Super Minions because of their slow movement speed, and can allow the rest of your defenses an opening to attack especially once the Super Minion runs out of Long Shot attacks.

## Super Bowler  (rev 623122)
- info: Preferred Target: None; Attack Type: Area Splash 0.6 Tile Radius (Ground Only); Housing Space: 30; Movement Speed: 14; Attack Speed: 2.2s; Range: 3 tiles from first target; Special Ability: Triple Strike
- info: Boost Cost: 25,000; Boost Duration: 3d; Bowler Level Required: 4
- stats: Level | Damage per Second | Damage per Attack | Hitpoints
    7 | 220 | 484 | 2,300
    8 | 240 | 528 | 2,600
    9 | 270 | 594 | 3,000
    10 | 310 | 682 | 3,700
  * The Super Bowler resembles a larger Bowler, except he has an orange glove on his left hand, loincloth, sash and mask, a maroon belt with the Super Troop logo on it, and a pair of sandals with taupe soles and golden straps.
  * The Super Bowler throws enlarged spiky purple boulders that are able to bounce thrice (compared to a normal Bowler whose boulders bounce twice), doing splash damage with each impact. Successive bounces occur 3 tiles apart.
  * Super Bowlers have no preferred target when attacking; they will simply attack the closest building . However, if they become aware of enemy Clan Castle troops , Heroes , or Skeleton Trap skeletons (either by being attacked themselves or by being near another friendly troop under attack), and can fight back against them, they will leave their previously targeted building and instead engage the enemy troops. Once all of the nearby enemy troops are defeated, the Super Bowlers will proceed to attack the nearest structure.
  * You can also use the Super Bowlers as a Queen Walk by using Healers and the Grand Warden to assist them into the base. This is quite effective because of their decent health and long-range, being able to target core defenses from a distance. However, this requires support and precise timing, as they are vulnerable to traps and high burst damage.
  * Druids can also be an alternative if you have them because they have a much higher healing than that of Healers, their healing can bounce to the other Super Bowlers if they are close enough together, and cannot be targeted by Air Defenses, however they have a limited time to heal. Additionally, the resulting Bears (after the Druid's human form expires) will also tank for the Super Bowlers, greatly improving their survivability.
  * If two 3x3 buildings are spaced apart one or two tiles from each other, and the Super Bowler targets one of them from full range, he can hit the second building twice with the boulder's second and third bounces. This can prove especially useful against high-hitpoint buildings if they are this second building in question. Conversely, however, if the high-hitpoint building is first in line, the Super Bowler's subsequent bounces may be wasted once the building behind is destroyed. However, this can be helpful if you want to destroy defenses at the back of the resource storages.
  * Conversely, however, if the high-hitpoint building is first in line, the Super Bowler's subsequent bounces may be wasted once the building behind is destroyed. However, this can be helpful if you want to destroy defenses at the back of the resource storages.
  * However, this can be helpful if you want to destroy defenses at the back of the resource storages.
  * You can replace Bowlers with Super Bowlers in Yeti BoBat, Witch BoBat and P.E.K.K.A BoBat strategies because the Super Bowler has more hitpoints and has a greater reach than Bowlers. However, five Bowlers greatly outdamage a Super Bowler when dealing with a single target and/or whenever the third bounce does not hit anything.
  * As the main offensive power of the Super Bowler lies in his attack's triple bounces, he is often used together with Rage Spells . With the damage boost of the Rage Spell, Super Bowlers can quickly make short work of any areas with a high density of structures. This, combined with the Grand Warden's Eternal Tome ability and Freeze Spells to reduce the incoming damage from high-damage defenses for a short time, will allow the Super Bowlers to easily take out a huge portion of the enemy's base, provided if the base is compact enough and if the Super Bowlers stay in the Grand Warden's aura.
  * The Super Bowlers pair well with the Grand Warden along with the Life Aura and Rage Gem, since the Rage Gem increases their damage output and gives the Super Bowler a decent amount of health. The Rage Gem can be replaced with the Healing Tome if they need more health than damage or if you are carrying a Rage Spell. If you want to protect your Super Bowlers from severe damage, you can use the Eternal Tome and replace the Life Gem or Rage Gem.
  * Be careful of early Town Hall weapon activations, as due to the projectile's long bounce range, it can mess up your attack if not done correctly.
  * Because of his relatively low damage and hitpoints for his housing space and his inability to target air troops, the Super Bowler is not that good at defense. However, he does splash damage (like the Bowler) and has approximately four times the hitpoints of a normal Bowler, which means it can tank more shots. So swarming or Witch based attacks may be crippled by Super Bowlers, though this merit does not make them the best choice for defense. Also, Super Bowlers cannot target air units.
  * The Super Bowler also attacks slowly, so you can try to overwhelm him first by using swarms to deal with him. Also, he costs 30 housing space, so he is not a good defending troop at all, other than to distract troops for a while.
  * The Super Bowler is very weak to high-damage defenses such as the Monolith , Ricochet Cannon and single-target Inferno Towers . To prevent this, either use Freeze Spells or Overgrowth Spells to disable them, or use Invisibility Spells to turn them invisible.
  * Clan Castle troops can easily destroy the attacking Super Bowlers, especially defensive aerial units such as Rocket Balloons due to being unable to target them. For ground Clan Castle units, strong units such as P.E.K.K.As can deal devastating amounts of damage if the attacker does react quickly enough.

## Super Valkyrie  (rev 622824)
- info: Preferred Target: None; Attack Type: Area Splash (Ground Only); Housing Space: 20; Movement Speed: 24; Attack Speed: 1.1s; Range: 0.6 tiles; Special Ability: Farewell Gift

  * The first attack of the Super Valkyrie is much slower that those of the regular Valkyrie's, but is compensated by attacking faster than the latter after every subsequent attack.
  * The Super Valkyrie's Rage Spell is identical to a level 4 Rage Spell in terms of damage increase and speed increase. However, her Rage Spell affects a smaller area (4 tile radius, compared to the Rage Spell's 5) and lasts shorter than a regular Rage Spell. The Rage Spell can also affect defenses, though the potency of it on defenses is +60% damage, the same as with Rage Spells dropped by a Rage Spell Tower or Smasher .
  * Super Valkyries have no preferred target when attacking; they will simply attack the closest building . However, if they become aware of enemy Clan Castle troops , Heroes , or Skeleton Trap skeletons (either by being attacked themselves or by being near another friendly troop under attack), and can fight back against them, they will leave their previously targeted building and instead engage the enemy troops. Once all of the nearby enemy troops are defeated, the Super Valkyries will proceed to attack the nearest structure.
  * Alternatively, you can use her as a frontliner to power other troops or by using a siege machine and strategically timing the Super Valkyrie's death, you can use the Rage Spell to power through the core of a base and even mitigate damage caused by Poison Bombs at Town Halls 14 to 16.
  * The Super Valkyrie can also be used in Queen Charges to tank for the Queen, and the dropped Rage Spell can help power the Queen Charge. However, for this to be effective, the Super Valkyrie must be in a position where she dies to boost both the Archer Queen and Healers, otherwise, either of the two aforementioned will not be raged up.
  * However, for this to be effective, the Super Valkyrie must be in a position where she dies to boost both the Archer Queen and Healers, otherwise, either of the two aforementioned will not be raged up.

## Ice Hound  (rev 622637)
- info: Preferred Target: Air Defense; Attack Type: Melee (Ground Only); Housing Space: 40; Movement Speed: 20; Attack Speed: 2s; Range: 0.75 tiles; Special Ability: Dish Served Cold
- info: Boost Cost: 25,000; Boost Duration: 3d; Lava Hound Level Required: 5
- stats: Level | Damage per Second | Damage per Attack | Freeze Time when Destroyed | Ice Pups Spawned / On Offense | Ice Pups Spawned / On Defense | Hitpoints | Town Hall Level Required
    5 | 10 | 20 | 3.75s | 10 | 10 | 9,500 | 12
    6 | 15 | 30 | 4.25s | 12 | 11 | 10,000 | 13
    7 | 20 | 40 | 4.75s | 14 | 12 | 10,500 | 16
    8 | 25 | 50 | 5.25s | 16 | 13 | 11,500 | 18
  * The Ice Hound is similar to a Lava Hound, except with higher hitpoints, spawning less pups compared to the Lava Hound, and an ability to chill targets that he hits, which slows afflicted targets down just like the Ice Wizard and Ice Golem . Upon death, he freezes the surrounding area like an Ice Golem , and releases several Ice Pups . The Ice Hound also does a little less damage than the Lava Hound .
  * Make sure that Ice Hound do not explodes into Ice Pups prematurely, because otherwise his Freeze upon death and his high tanking potential will be next to useless.
  * Ice Hounds can be used very similarly to Lava Hounds in LavaLoon attacks. They can shield for the Balloons and also destroy enemy Heroes with their Ice Pups once popped, and the freeze upon death can help freeze defenses and reduce the damage taken by your damage dealers.
  * Like the Ice Golem , an Ice Hound can stop a single-target Inferno Tower from harming powerful troops. However, it will still deal that devastating third-stage damage, albeit just slower, so still watch out for that!
  * Even though he has more hitpoints, Ice Hound should be treated more towards an air-Ice Golem than an air- Golem . This is because of his freezing effect and less Ice Pups, However, he boasts a lot of hitpoints, so he still has great tanking potential.
  * The Ice Hound and his pups can slow down defenses, acting as a great support and tank for many air troops and for many air-based attack.
  * Putting your single-target Inferno Towers closer to the outside could cause Ice Hounds to pop early and waste their ability. Keep in mind that this could weaken your base to things like Dragon attacks.
  * Make sure you destroy them as fast as possible, or else their freeze and his Ice Pups could be a threat to your village . As such, make sure you guard your Air Defenses and your single-target Inferno Towers so they do not get destroyed too quickly and letting the Ice Hound survive more.

  * Each Meteormite spawned through this attack starts with half the current health of the Meteor Golem, rounded down to the nearest 1% of Meteormite health. Thus a damaged Meteor Golem will spawn damaged Meteormites upon splitting up.
  * Each time they recombine, they will become immune to damage for a short time.
  * The total health of the Meteor Golem is determined by the health of both Meteormites, and will be subtracted if one or both of them take (s) damage. For example, if the Meteor Golem launches a Meteormite and takes another one from the other Meteor Golem, the total health of that merged unit may be higher or lower, even the level will either be lower or higher. As mentioned before, when they throw again, the hitpoints of the Meteor Golem will equally be split in half, even if the Meteormites were damaged previously before merging in the first place.
  * For example, if the Meteor Golem launches a Meteormite and takes another one from the other Meteor Golem, the total health of that merged unit may be higher or lower, even the level will either be lower or higher. As mentioned before, when they throw again, the hitpoints of the Meteor Golem will equally be split in half, even if the Meteormites were damaged previously before merging in the first place.
  * Its Meteormites can jump over Walls without the use of Jump Spells , but Meteor Golems themselves cannot.
  * Meteor Golems have no preferred target when attacking; they will simply attack the closest building . However, if they become aware of enemy Clan Castle troops , Heroes , or Skeleton Trap skeletons (either by being attacked themselves or by being near another friendly troop under attack), and can fight back against them, they will leave their previously targeted building and instead engage the enemy troops. Once all of the nearby enemy troops are defeated, the Meteor Golems will proceed to attack the nearest structure.
  * Using Meteor Golems is considered to be one of the best attack strategies in the game, especially at Town Halls 17-18, in tandem with the Warden Charge ( Fireball and Rage Gem equipped). Once the funnel is set, the Meteor Golems will wreak havoc on to the base, provided if they are supported by units that target air, like the Archer Queen . However, since they are slow-moving, it may lead to a time fail if the attack is not fast enough, so use cleanup troops like Archers , Minions , and Wizards .
  * However, since they are slow-moving, it may lead to a time fail if the attack is not fast enough, so use cleanup troops like Archers , Minions , and Wizards .
  * An alternative to this strategy is to use the Royal Champion Charge to destroy defenses, but this is often risky because it may result in a time fail.
  * The throw damage can be boosted by the Rage Spell, dealing more damage. If paired with Healers, the Meteor Golems themselves can also be regenerated significantly which also recovers both of the Meteormites' health, but when seperated, the Healers can heal the resulting Meteor Golem more effectively than when it is alone.
  * If paired with Healers, the Meteor Golems themselves can also be regenerated significantly which also recovers both of the Meteormites' health, but when seperated, the Healers can heal the resulting Meteor Golem more effectively than when it is alone.
  * Ice Golems can prove effective against Meteor Golems as they can freeze them for a short time, and results in a time fail. When they are deployed from the Clan Castle, use a Totem Spell to distract the Ice Golem from attacking them. Skeleton Spells can also work in this regard.
  * Defending against Meteor Golems can be quite tricky, especially since their Meteormites can jump over Walls .
  * Defending Heroes such as the Barbarian King can be very effective when countering Meteormites, as he has a lot of hitpoints, giving more time for defenses to defeat them, and can even disable their merging mechanics if they get to close, forcing them to attack him. Similarly, Ice Golems prove to be a valuable counter to Meteor Golems and their Meteormites, as they can also freeze them for a short time. Other units can also distract them for a short time, as they have a slow attack speed, like Skeletons from Skeleton Traps .
  * Similarly, Ice Golems prove to be a valuable counter to Meteor Golems and their Meteormites, as they can also freeze them for a short time. Other units can also distract them for a short time, as they have a slow attack speed, like Skeletons from Skeleton Traps .
  * When paired with defenses as the Rage Spell Tower, they can quickly ramp up damage to the Meteor Golems and Meteormites, eventually destroying them;
  * If it is a Poison Spell Tower, it can help to slow down the advance of Meteor Golems, allowing more time for defenses to defeat them; or
  * If it is an Invisibility Spell Tower, when a building is hit, it turns invisible along with the main defenses inside, forcing the Meteor Golems to retarget somewhere else that deals less damage while those inside can easily deal with them while defenses stay invisible.

## Ruin Witch  (rev 625328)
- info: Preferred Target: Rubble; Housing Space: 26; Movement Speed: 12; Dark Barracks Level Required: 13; Range: 0.8 tiles
- stats: Level | Ruin Knight Level | Maximum Ruin Knights Summoned | Hitpoints | Damage per Second* | Damage per Hit* | Research Cost | Research Time | Laboratory Level Required

  * When deployed, she does not do anything and will just stand still. However, if a building is destroyed, she will begin to move towards the debris, ignoring everything else. She cannot target destroyed buildings surrounded by closed wall compartments and will ignore them. Invisible or overgrown rubble also works the same.
  * She cannot target destroyed buildings surrounded by closed wall compartments and will ignore them. Invisible or overgrown rubble also works the same.
  * If the Ruin Witch manages to summon ten Ruin Knights, her vacuum will self-destruct, destroying the Ruin Witch after her summon animation completes.
  * On defense, the Ruin Witch does not attempt to vacuum any rubble destroyed by attackers, but she will use her vacuum to attack as a single-target ground-only defender. This attack deals low damage per second (25-28 depending on the level), making her a poor defender.
  * Never deploy Ruin Witches first - they cannot attack, leaving them susceptible to single-target damage, especially from Monoliths , single-target Inferno Towers and Revenge Towers , since they will defeat them quickly.
  * Defending against Ruin Witches can be tricky, but the player can prevent them from happening by using walls to redirect her to other buildings, mostly common in anti- Giant Arrow - Rocket Backpack and Earthquake Spell bases.
  * The player should never use the Ruin Witch in the Clan Castle, as she will do nothing but tank the damage. She will try to attack the attacking army, but will ultimately fail because of her very low damage and can target only one unit at a time. Therefore, replace her with other Clan Castle units like Ice Golems and Furnaces .
  * * The Ruin Witch deals very small damage per second using her vacuum only when defending.


# Heroes

## Barbarian King  (rev 625673)
- info: Preferred Target: None; Attack Type: Melee (Ground Only); Movement Speed: 16; Attack Speed: 1.2s; Range: 1 tile; Search Radius: 9 tiles

  * "Biggest and bravest of all barbarians , this colossal menace soaks up huge amounts of damage and smashes anything in his path with his sword. He guards his territory fiercely when defending."
  * The Barbarian King has very high health and a high damage output so he is best used as a tank and a brute force. Deploy him first to distract the defenses then deploy lower health units (like the Archer Queen ) to do the damage behind walls. However, at lower levels, like Town Halls 4 to 9, it is most likely that players might want him inside the base since Heroes are good damage dealers. Another way to use him is for funneling, as is very clear where to funnel, which can be easier depending on his Equipment.
  * When unlocked, he starts with the Barbarian Puppet and Rage Vial. The Barbarian Puppet allows the Barbarian King to summon a large number of raged Barbarians and an increased health recovery. The level of the Barbarians matches the level researched by the player, while the rage increases their damage and movement speed. It gives a passive increase to max health. The Rage Vial enrages the Barbarian King with his ability, increasing his damage and movement speed, and grants a vastly increased health recovery. It gives a passive increase to damage.
  * The Barbarian Puppet allows the Barbarian King to summon a large number of raged Barbarians and an increased health recovery. The level of the Barbarians matches the level researched by the player, while the rage increases their damage and movement speed. It gives a passive increase to max health.
  * The Rage Vial enrages the Barbarian King with his ability, increasing his damage and movement speed, and grants a vastly increased health recovery. It gives a passive increase to damage.
  * When the Blacksmith is constructed and is upgraded to level 3, it unlocks the Earthquake Boots and Vampstache respectively. The Earthquake Boots deals damage similar to an Earthquake Spell , except that it destroys walls instantly and damages all ground units in its radius. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health. The Vampstache passively restores a small fraction of the King's health every time he lands an attack. It gives a passive increase to damage and attack speed.
  * The Earthquake Boots deals damage similar to an Earthquake Spell , except that it destroys walls instantly and damages all ground units in its radius. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * The Vampstache passively restores a small fraction of the King's health every time he lands an attack. It gives a passive increase to damage and attack speed.
  * The Giant Gauntlet makes the King grow gigantic, allowing him to deal splash damage, hit buildings behind Walls , and reduces damage taken for a period of time. It gives a passive increase to damage and grants self healing.
  * The Spiky Ball makes the King kick a ricocheting ball which bounces to a number of buildings, dealing massive damage. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * The Snake Bracelet passively generates Snakes whenever the King takes damage up to a limit, which can jump over Walls. It gives a passive increase to max health and attack speed.
  * The Stick Horse allows the King the jump over Walls, and attack and move faster for a while when deployed. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * The ability attributes (speed increases, damage increases, etc.) of his default equipment are unaffected by this scaling, only its passive stats.
  * He is best used with support from ranged troops, such as the Archer , Wizard , Witch , and the Headhunter to kill air troops and to deal damage behind. However, make sure that the ranged troops are protected from strong splash defenses such as the Scattershot , Mortar , and the Eagle Artillery , otherwise they will be taken out quickly.
  * However, make sure that the ranged troops are protected from strong splash defenses such as the Scattershot , Mortar , and the Eagle Artillery , otherwise they will be taken out quickly.
  * He is also good for helping to destroy the remaining buildings of a village, after most or all defenses have been destroyed. However, he has a slow movement speed, so you might have to speed him up if you want an effective cleanup procedure, either with his ability or other spells.
  * However, he has a slow movement speed, so you might have to speed him up if you want an effective cleanup procedure, either with his ability or other spells.
  * The Barbarian King can be easily swarmed by a group of Archers or Barbarians , as he only attacks one target at a time and has a somewhat slow attack speed against swarms. However, the Giant Gauntlet ability can negate this, since it allows the King to do splash damage and take less damage by himself.

  * The Archer Queen is the second Hero unlocked at Hero Hall Level 2, which requires Town Hall Level 8. She is essentially a bigger and much more powerful version of the Archer . In addition to possessing much higher damage and hitpoints, the Archer Queen also shoots 1.5 tiles farther than a normal Archer (5 tiles instead of 3.5 tiles).
  * The Archer Queen shoots three rapid arrows per shot. However, that is more of a visual feature, because these arrows deal damage to a single target.
  * When unlocked, she starts with the Archer Puppet and Invisibility Vial. The Archer Puppet allows the Archer Queen to summon a large number of invisible Archers and an increase health recovery. The level of the Archers matches the level researched by the player. It gives a passive increase to damage. The Invisibility Vial turns the Archer Queen invisible and gives her significantly increased damage for the duration. It gives a passive increase to maximum health.
  * The Archer Puppet allows the Archer Queen to summon a large number of invisible Archers and an increase health recovery. The level of the Archers matches the level researched by the player. It gives a passive increase to damage.
  * The Invisibility Vial turns the Archer Queen invisible and gives her significantly increased damage for the duration. It gives a passive increase to maximum health.
  * Upgrading the Blacksmith to level 2 and 5 unlocks the Giant Arrow and Healer Puppet respectively. The Giant Arrow launches a large arrow that travels the whole village, dealing damage on the way. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health. The Healer Puppet summons Healers, with the number of Healers is dependent on the level of this Equipment. It gives a passive increase to her maximum health and grants her self healing.
  * The Giant Arrow launches a large arrow that travels the whole village, dealing damage on the way. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * The Healer Puppet summons Healers, with the number of Healers is dependent on the level of this Equipment. It gives a passive increase to her maximum health and grants her self healing.
  * The Frozen Arrow passively makes her attacks slow down defenses, defending Heroes and troops. It gives a passive increase to damage.
  * The Magic Mirror summons clone(s) of herself, provides a brief invisibility, and grants an increased health recovery with her ability. It gives a passive increase to max health.
  * The Action Figure summons a Giant Giant to fight by the Archer Queen's side. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health and grants her self healing.
  * The Monolith Arrow passively makes her attacks deal bonus damage based off the target's maximum health. This bonus damage decreases the more troops are deployed. it also gives a passive hitpoint increase and grants damage reduction.
  * The five Home Village Heroes serve different roles in raids. The Barbarian King is the strength specialist, the Minion Prince is the air specialist, the Archer Queen is the speed specialist, the Grand Warden is the support specialist and the Royal Champion is the offense specialist. Deploy her behind the Barbarian King as he tanks and deals the damage while the Archer Queen does necessary damage in order to destroy certain defenses by her speed.
  * She has a better range than an Archer (5 tiles instead of 3.5), so a good strategy is to place a group of Giants , P.E.K.K.As , Golems , or the Barbarian King as a meat shield, then a group of Archers, and finally the Archer Queen. This minimizes the chance of her being defeated or even hit and may allow you to use her many times in a row without waiting for her to regenerate.
  * A related version of the Queen Walk is the Queen Charge, where instead of being used for funneling, the Queen and Healers target the core of the base, destroying key defenses. This usually involves the use of Wall Breakers , their super form , or Jump Spells in addition to other funneling troops (such as Baby Dragons ) to ensure she enters the base, and then using her abilities to wipe defenses.
  * Seeking Air Mines can also counter this. Periodically place some Balloons near the Healers to try and soak them up. These Balloons are often called "coco loons". Try to bring about 3 or 4. Any extra ones may also be used like normal Balloons. However, the Seeking Air Mine strategy does not work on Druids, since they are ground units and will not trigger these aerial traps in any way, at the cost of being vulnerable to defenses and traps that target ground units.
  * However, the Seeking Air Mine strategy does not work on Druids, since they are ground units and will not trigger these aerial traps in any way, at the cost of being vulnerable to defenses and traps that target ground units.
  * The Unicorn can be used in place of a Healer or Druid. However, in a lot of strategies, you see them both used at the same time for maximum healing to the Archer Queen, which increases her sustainability.

  * As a supporting Hero, the Grand Warden will attack the building being attacked by the highest number/troop space troop and will continue to attack that building until it is destroyed.
  * He can be set to Air or Ground mode; he flies in Air Mode and walks in Ground Mode. He can bypass Walls regardless of the mode he is set to; however, he will attack a Wall if the majority of your nearby housing space is doing so as well.
  * He can bypass Walls regardless of the mode he is set to; however, he will attack a Wall if the majority of your nearby housing space is doing so as well.
  * When unlocked, he starts with the Eternal Tome and Life Gem. The Eternal Tome lets the Grand Warden make himself and all attacking units in his radius invincible for several seconds. It does not have any passive stat increase. The Life Gem passively increases the health and max health of other attacking units within his radius by a percentage of their max health up to a cap. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.

  * The Life Gem passively increases the health and max health of other attacking units within his radius by a percentage of their max health up to a cap. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * Upgrading the Blacksmith to level 4 and 6 unlocks the Rage Gem and Healing Tome respectively. The Rage Gem passively increases the damage or healing of other attacking units within his radius by a percentage. It gives a passive increase to damage and attack speed. The Healing Tome lets the Grand Warden heal himself and all units within his radius over time. It gives a passive increase to max health.
  * The Rage Gem passively increases the damage or healing of other attacking units within his radius by a percentage. It gives a passive increase to damage and attack speed.
  * The Healing Tome lets the Grand Warden heal himself and all units within his radius over time. It gives a passive increase to max health.
  * The Fireball has the Grand Warden throw a fireball at the nearest defense, dealing massive damage within a large radius. It gives a passive increase to his damage.
  * The Lavaloon Puppet summons one to two Lavaloons. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * The Heroic Torch lets the Grand Warden give all units within his radius damage resistance, increased movement speed, and the ability to walk through walls. It gives a passive increase to his damage, max health, and health recovery.
  * If the ability has not been used before the Grand Warden is about to be defeated, it will by default automatically activate. However, this feature can be toggled off in the settings (by turning off "Automatically use Hero Abilities on KO"). This ability can still activate even if the Warden would be one-shot by an attack (such as by a Seeking Air Mine ); the Warden will activate his ability and survive with 1 hitpoint.
  * This ability can still activate even if the Warden would be one-shot by an attack (such as by a Seeking Air Mine ); the Warden will activate his ability and survive with 1 hitpoint.
  * The Grand Warden has two AI modes during an attack based on a hidden attribute called Warden Weight. If the total Warden Weight is under 20, he remains in regular target mode, where he targets the nearest building to him, ignoring nearby friendly troops. If the total Warden Weight reaches 20 or higher, he instantly switches to group target mode, where the Grand Warden locks onto whichever building is being targeted by the majority or heaviest concentration of nearby troops.
  * If the total Warden Weight is under 20, he remains in regular target mode, where he targets the nearest building to him, ignoring nearby friendly troops.
  * If the total Warden Weight reaches 20 or higher, he instantly switches to group target mode, where the Grand Warden locks onto whichever building is being targeted by the majority or heaviest concentration of nearby troops.

  * The Royal Champion is the fifth Hero unlocked once the Hero Hall reaches Level 7, which is available at Town Hall level 13. She has moderately high damage per second, moderate health, and targets defenses . She can also jump over Walls , much like the Hog Rider and Headhunter .
  * The Royal Champion has moderately high health, but high damage so she is best used as a ranged damage dealer just like the Archer Queen . Most likely, you want to protect your Royal Champion so that she can destroy defenses.

  * Upgrading the Blacksmith to level 7 and 8 unlocks the Hog Rider Puppet and Haste Vial respectively. The Hog Rider Puppet summons Hog Riders, provides a brief invisibility, and grants an increased health recovery with her ability. It gives a passive increase to max health. The Haste Vial greatly increases her movement speed and attack speed for a short duration with her ability. It gives a passive increase to damage and attack speed.
  * The Hog Rider Puppet summons Hog Riders, provides a brief invisibility, and grants an increased health recovery with her ability. It gives a passive increase to max health.
  * The Haste Vial greatly increases her movement speed and attack speed for a short duration with her ability. It gives a passive increase to damage and attack speed.
  * The Rocket Spear massively increases her attack range and damage and grants a small splash radius for a few attacks with her ability. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * The Electro Boots passively create an electric aura similar to that of the Electro Titan's , dealing damage to nearby enemy units and buildings. It gives a passive increase to max health and grant her self healing.
  * The Frost Flake shoots multiple snow flakes at the closest defense, freezes them for a short while, and deals a small amount of damage. It gives a passive increase to max health.
  * The five Home Village Heroes serve different roles in raids. The Barbarian King is the strength specialist, the Minion Prince is the air specialist, the Archer Queen is the speed specialist, the Grand Warden is the support specialist and the Royal Champion is the offense specialist. Deploy her behind the Barbarian King as he tanks and deals the damage while the Royal Champion destroys defenses which are distracted by the King, improving both of their survivabilities.
  * The Seeking Shield, when equipped, can destroy weak defenses such as the Mortar and Hidden Tesla in one hit. This allows the Royal Champion to set up funnels. Sending her like this without much backup is known as a suicide (or sui) Champion.
  * A variation of this uses Druids instead of Healers, since the former are ground units, but they only heal her for a limited time.
  * Another version of the Royal Champion Walk is to use Invisibility Spells instead of Healers to destroy important defenses, known as the Royal Champion Charge.
  * Send her in with the main pack of a Hybrid ( Hog Riders and Miners ) as they will tank for her and she will deal massive damage to the defenses. She also can attack defending Heroes that are attacking the pack, and then will also follow the pack due to also targeting defenses. She will also protect the pack from defending aerial Clan Castle troops.
  * Often it is better to send her later in the attack, where the main army is starting to lose power getting to the backend defenses to take out said defenses. Deploy her when some of the defenses are attacking the main army. Sending her in too early may result in her quick death. One way to do this is when you have a Siege Barracks in your army, fill that with Hog Riders and then when they deploy, place the Royal Champion with them.

  * The Minion Prince is an aerial Hero who attacks all targets from long range that was introduced on November 25, 2024, with the Town Hall 17 update. He is unlocked by upgrading the Hero Hall to level 3, which requires Town Hall 9.
  * The Minion Prince will defend the village when assigned to a Hero Banner . The Minion Prince only defends his territory around his banner and will retreat if lured too far out. He does not need to be defeated for an attack to end in a 3-star. When defending, the Minion Prince will fly, making him unreachable by units that only target ground such as the Barbarian King.
  * When defending, the Minion Prince will fly, making him unreachable by units that only target ground such as the Barbarian King.
  * When unlocked, he starts with the Henchmen Puppet and the Dark Orb. The Henchmen Puppet summons a pair of Henchmen , provides a brief invisibility, and grants an increased health recovery. It gives a passive increase to damage. The Dark Orb launches an ice crystal that travels forward from the Minion Prince and affects all enemy targets in its vicinity as it travels. Affected targets take some damage and are slowed down temporarily. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * The Henchmen Puppet summons a pair of Henchmen , provides a brief invisibility, and grants an increased health recovery. It gives a passive increase to damage.
  * The Dark Orb launches an ice crystal that travels forward from the Minion Prince and affects all enemy targets in its vicinity as it travels. Affected targets take some damage and are slowed down temporarily. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * Upgrading the Blacksmith to level 3 and 5 unlocks the Metal Pants and Noble Iron respectively. The Metal Pants reduces damage taken for a period of time and grants a vastly increased health recovery. It gives a passive increase to max health. The Noble Iron allows him to start the battle with several long-ranged attacks that deal extra damage. It gives a passive increases to attack speed.
  * The Metal Pants reduces damage taken for a period of time and grants a vastly increased health recovery. It gives a passive increase to max health.
  * The Noble Iron allows him to start the battle with several long-ranged attacks that deal extra damage. It gives a passive increases to attack speed.
  * Additionally, there are Epic rarity Hero Equipment which can be purchased with event medals during certain events or gems with the Trader . The Dark Crown passively buffs the Minion Prince's damage and max health as every 60 housing space worth of friendly units die, up to three times. It gives a passive increase to max health and attack speed. The Meteor Staff passively summons a meteor periodically, targeting the closest defense. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * The Dark Crown passively buffs the Minion Prince's damage and max health as every 60 housing space worth of friendly units die, up to three times. It gives a passive increase to max health and attack speed.
  * The Meteor Staff passively summons a meteor periodically, targeting the closest defense. It gives a passive increase to damage and max health.
  * If the ability has not been used before the Minion Prince is about to be defeated, it will by default automatically activate. However, this feature can be toggled off in the settings (by turning off "Automatically use Hero Abilities on KO"). This ability can still activate even if the Minion Prince would be one-shot by an attack (such as by a Seeking Air Mine ); he will activate his ability and survive with 1 hitpoint.
  * This ability can still activate even if the Minion Prince would be one-shot by an attack (such as by a Seeking Air Mine ); he will activate his ability and survive with 1 hitpoint.
  * He should be deployed behind the main army of higher health aerial units like Dragons , Dragon Riders , Electro Dragons, Lava Hounds , and Baby Dragons . They have a shorter range than Minion Prince and can tank for him as he deals massive amount of damage. Placing him together with Electro Dragons is not ideal, as he may take down buildings that allows the chain lightning to keep going. He should be placed to the the side of the main attack to funnel for the Electro Dragons.
  * Placing him together with Electro Dragons is not ideal, as he may take down buildings that allows the chain lightning to keep going. He should be placed to the the side of the main attack to funnel for the Electro Dragons.
  * Be mindful of the defending Minion Prince, as he is untargetable by units which target ground. Use Wizards or the Archer Queen to knock him out of battle.
  * As a defending Hero, make sure to place him near the center of the base, as he will distract aerial units due to his high hitpoints.

## Dragon Duke  (rev 625384)
- info: Preferred Target: None; Attack Type: Single Target; Movement Speed: 20; Attack Speed: 1.2s; Range: 0.3 tiles; Search Radius: 10 tiles; Passive Ability: Royal Rampage; Ability Damage Increase: 100%; Ability Attack Speed Increase: 50%
- stats: Level | Damage per Second | Damage per Hit | Hitpoints | Health Recovery | Upgrade Cost | Upgrade Time | Hero Hall Level Required
    22 | 388 | 465.6 | 10,675 | 4,500 | 420,000 | 8d | 12
